[00:34:39.886] My name is Sam Toya, and I'm here on behalf of the Illinois Restaurant
[00:34:43.246] Association. I'd like to start out by thanking Alders Burnett,
[00:34:47.366] Quinn, Villegas, and Nugent for their tireless work on the tip
[00:34:51.147] credit issue. I want to be clear about one thing.
[00:34:54.766] We do not support the elimination of the tip credit.
[00:34:58.066] We believe the tip credit is an important part of our industry
[00:35:01.446] compensation model and one that benefits many tipped employees.
[00:35:06.206] The proposal we put forward during negotiations would have guaranteed
[00:35:10.006] tip workers a wage equal to 124% of
[00:35:13.926] the standard minimum wage.
[00:35:16.256] We believe that was a meaningful step forward toward more predictable pay,
[00:35:20.606] which has been one of the main concerns raised by supporters eliminating tip
[00:35:24.166] credit. We felt this was pragmatic and progressive.
[00:35:27.526] Under our proposal, operators who committed to the higher
[00:35:31.146] guaranteed wage would've been able to maintain the 24% tip credit.
[00:35:35.526] We continue to believe that was their policy.
[00:35:38.746] If tips were strong, the worker benefited.
[00:35:41.346] Tips were low, the employer had to make up the difference.
[00:35:44.376] The employer could keep the tip credit only by accepting the
[00:35:48.086] risk of guaranteeing the worker more than the minimum wage.
[00:35:51.466] Again, more than the minimum wage.
[00:35:53.866] That approach was simple, clear, and balanced.
[00:35:57.066] It showed that preserving the tip credit and protecting tip workers do
[00:36:00.846] not have to be in conflict. That said, we recognize
[00:36:04.886] where the discussion stands today.
[00:36:07.146] This ordinance is not the ideal outcome for our industry, but it is more
[00:36:10.686] workable and less harmful than allowing the current phase-out to
[00:36:14.406] continue. The most important part of this ordinance is that it
[00:36:17.926] extends the current 24% tip credit allowance through
[00:36:22.206] June 30th, 2028, and slowly phases it out until
[00:36:26.146] 2030, except for the smaller operators.
[00:36:30.186] The additional time matters. Chicago restaurant industry is in crisis.
[00:36:34.426] According to the Federal Bureau of Labor and Statistics, Chicago's
[00:36:38.366] full-service restaurant industry lost 21 jobs in
[00:36:42.126] 2025. Additionally, in the first half of 2025
[00:36:46.106] alone, nearly 500 restaurants closedRestaurants are
[00:36:49.970] facing increased costs for labor, food, rent, and insurance,
[00:36:53.870] utilities, taxes, and fees. Operators need time to
[00:36:57.670] adjust so they can keep their doors open and their staff employed.
[00:37:01.750] Predictability matters for workers, too.
[00:37:04.330] Abrupt changes can lead to reduced hours, fewer shifts, higher
[00:37:08.310] menu prices, more service charges, and fewer opportunities for tipped
[00:37:12.290] employees.
[00:37:13.670] So while this ordinance is not perfect, it gives the industry more time to adjust
[00:37:17.510] and better manage increased labor costs.
[00:37:20.330] For those reasons, while we will always oppose the elimination of tip credit, we
[00:37:24.150] can support this ordinance as a compromise. Thank you.

[00:37:54.689] This morning, we're urging the city council to act on three fronts.
[00:37:59.150] We support Hazel Johnson Cumulative Impact Ordinance because our community in
[00:38:02.630] Southeast knows what it means when pollution gets zoned into neighborhood
[00:38:06.330] generations after generations. Landfill after landfill,
[00:38:10.350] truck depot after truck depot, each one approved in isolation as if
[00:38:14.170] cumulative weight on our air, our health, and our children doesn't exist.
[00:38:18.650] Cumulative Impact Ordinance fixes that.
[00:38:20.880] It requires the city to assess the full burden of a community already
[00:38:24.730] carrying before adding more.
[00:38:28.670] Putting health data and community voices into the permitting process before the
[00:38:32.430] damage is done. And it honors the legacy of Hazel
[00:38:36.170] Johnson, who spent her life naming what is happening to us as environmental
[00:38:40.230] racism.
[00:38:41.560] And let's be clear, people should not have to leave their homes to find a clean
[00:38:44.950] environment. So the Southeast Side is our home.
[00:38:48.130] These are our blocks, our churches, our schools, our families.
[00:38:50.540] We're not asking to be relocated. We're asking for the same clean air and healthy

[01:25:12.654] veterans are more than twice as likely to be diagnosed as other
[01:25:16.614] Americans.

[06:09:33.940] I want to start by acknowledging that I understand that some may feel that this
[06:09:37.460] ordinance does not solve the entire problem of rising
[06:09:41.180] extremism.
[06:09:43.080] We have one proposal here, but one proposal rarely
[06:09:47.039] accomplishes that. But that cannot be an excuse for
[06:09:50.840] inaction in its entirety. This ordinance has been carefully
[06:09:54.160] workshopped for more than a year, with input from dozens of
[06:09:57.560] organizations, legal experts, and advocates as well, and it
[06:10:01.480] reflects extensive feedback and has earned the support from a broad
[06:10:05.460] coalition, including our police department, community leaders,
[06:10:09.440] and First Amendment advocates.
[06:10:11.609] Now, some have raised concerns that the proposal does not apply to every single
[06:10:15.280] employee or every elected official.
[06:10:18.260] But proposals like this must remain sensitive to distinct legal
[06:10:22.130] and constitutional considerations.
[06:10:25.000] Here, it's important to emphasize that members of our police department
[06:10:28.440] occupy a unique role. They carry government-issued weapons,
[06:10:32.720] they exercise the power of the state, and they are empowered and
[06:10:36.540] entrusted to enforce our laws every single day
[06:10:40.780] and in every single part of our city.
[06:10:43.700] This distinction matters.
[06:10:46.640] As for elected officials, removal is governed by state law, not
[06:10:50.520] local law, and it raises profound questions about
[06:10:54.100] democratic representation and the will of voters.
[06:10:58.100] These issues deserve serious deliberation and should not be
[06:11:01.960] invoked at the 11th hour as a reason to halt progress in its
[06:11:05.700] entirety. I want to emphasize that anti-government
[06:11:09.360] extremism has been a growing problem across our country
[06:11:13.160] for years.
[06:11:14.920] However, the Trump administration has been systematically rolling back
[06:11:18.660] progress in combating extremism and going well beyond the January 6th
[06:11:22.620] insurrection.
[06:11:24.109] The Trump administration, for example, has dismantled Biden-era
[06:11:28.000] directives to root out extremism in both our military
[06:11:31.860] and our federal law enforcementThe Trump administration has also cut
[06:11:35.904] funding for programs that track domestic terrorist threats
[06:11:39.684] from white supremacist groups and anti-government extremists.
[06:11:43.894] And most recently, the Trump administration is proposing to put together a
[06:11:47.654] $1.8 billion fund to compensate
[06:11:51.204] people who were investigated by the Department of Justice.
[06:11:55.544] It's important for us to recognize that our great city is not immune to these
[06:11:59.204] challenges either.
[06:12:01.084] In 2024, our Office of Inspector General found that the city's handling
[06:12:05.064] of extremism in its police ranks has fallen short of
[06:12:08.724] commitments made by city leadership.
[06:12:11.964] And in doing so, the office referenced current and
[06:12:15.424] former police officers' associations with the Proud Boys, the
[06:12:19.364] Three Percenters, and Oath Keepers.
[06:12:22.324] The office also recommended that city leaders craft and implement a
[06:12:26.124] plan to prevent, identify, and eliminate
[06:12:29.704] extremist and anti-government activities across the police department.
[06:12:35.124] The substitute ordinance before you aims to heed that very
[06:12:38.684] call by prohibiting CPD members from actively
[06:12:42.264] participating in extremist organizations.
